### [Fragments: September  8](https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-09-08.html)

**来源**: Martin Fowler

**摘要**: <p>Christian Catalini says we’re in a situation where we are <a href="https://catalini.com/ideas/economics-of-ai/">vastly reducing the cost of generating things, but not the cost of verifying them:</a>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>This explains why the first major AI products appeared in chat, image generation, and code assistance. Not because these were the hardest human problems, but because their outputs were relatively easy to inspect. A user can judge the tone of a message, look at an image, or run a test on a piece of code.
[…]
The old automation boundary was routine versus non-routine work. The new boundary is increasingly measurable versus non-measurable work.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>The issue is then over how well you can measure something. In our profession, we know there’s a big difference between how many lines of code we write and how productive we are, and we’ve seen a regular <a href="https://martinfowler.com/bliki/CannotMeasureProductivity.html">failure to understand how to measure productivity</a>. Too much of what makes work effective is subject to either slow feedback loops or assessments that require subtle judgment. The danger is that people use lots AI automation while using incomplete measurements of its effectiveness, leading to short-term dashboards going up, but disaster in longer time-scales. He refers to these illusory short-term gains as <strong>counterfeit utility</strong>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Scale this across companies and institutions and the result is a <strong>Hollow Economy</strong>: extraordinary measured activity sitting on top of weakening human capability, hidden technical debt, correlated errors, and outcomes that nobody can confidently stand behind.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>Another highlight in the article was his advice to “build a history of decisions, not a gallery of outputs”. The point is that with AI we can all build really impressive things, but our value lies in the judgment that we’ve formed. It reminds me of how math problems were marked at school. We weren’t just marked on getting the final answer, we were also marked based on our reasoning process.</p>

<p>He uses the OpenAI–Hugging Face incident as an illustration of this gap between generation and verification. He criticizes those who anthropomorphize the agents involved in the attack. By doing so we focus on the behavior of the AI agents, but instead we should focus on the financial incentives that created them and the environment they are operating in.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Labs are locked in a race. The training run is where the money goes, and RL optimizes exactly what you score. The runs were scored on capability. They were not scored on “did not poison the Artifactory cache.”</p>
</blockquote>

<p>I assert that the organizations that build and run agents are responsible for <em>everything</em> those agents do, whether that behavior is intended or emergent. If they reap counterfeit utility by neglecting verification, they must face consequences: legal, financial, and if necessary: criminal. To deal effectively with AI, we need to change the incentives involved to ensure people invest more in verification than they do in generation. Otherwise we are driving a car that has a powerful engine, but weak brakes.</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>Brian Cantrill relates how <a href="https://bcantrill.dtrace.org/2026/09/05/the-revolt-of-the-reader/">readers are exasperated with “writers” using LLMs</a>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>To those who read broadly, the hand of the LLM is so clear it’s as if the writer’s intellectual fly is open. In fact, it’s so jarring that I have to believe that those writing with LLMs are either not reading enough to see the LLM’s obvious structural tells — or (and?) they aren’t even reading their own content. (A confession: with particularly egregious pieces, I have fantasized about sentencing the author to read them aloud, certain that they themselves will be unable to endure the slop that they are foisting upon the rest of us.)</p>
</blockquote>

<p>He points out that readers do care about this, a survey found 78% of readers stop immediately once they sense something is the work a stochastic parrot, and 71% go on to blacklist the writer. It’s not the polish, it’s the authenticity that counts. Readers will always prefer the clumsy voice of the author over the gloss of an LLM’s whispering.</p>

<p>Cantrill reports good success with using <a href="https://www.pangram.com/blog/pangram-4-technical">Pangram</a> to detect AI writing. I confess I’m a bit wary, do I really trust anyone’s judgment to disentangle LLM-voice from changes in generation and context? Maybe people steeped in Silicon Valley culture authentically speak in LLM-voice these days. Sadly for them, to be misclassified by their readers as an LLM is just as bad as using the damn things.</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>One of the dirty non-secrets about LLMs is that they were trained on a vast corpus of writing, without consulting the authors of that writing to see if they were cool with it. Individual authors like me can’t do a great deal about it, so are easy to ignore, but music companies aren’t exactly known for taking this kind of thing lying down. So they are <a href="https://www.theguardian.com/business/2026/aug/31/aanthropic-sued-alleged-theft-songs-ai-train-claude">suing over the use song lyrics for LLM training</a>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Sony Music Publishing and Warner Chappell, music publishers who manage the copyright of songs on behalf of songwriters and composers, are seeking damages for alleged misuse of “tens of thousands” of copyrighted works by Anthropic.
[…]
The plaintiffs claim they are victims of “one of the largest and most blatant ongoing thefts of intellectual property in history”.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>Looking at it a broader societal point of view, there is an argument that the benefits of LLMs could be worth far more than any losses to us authors. But we should not forget that these tools are built on a foundation they used without our consent, and that should be taken into account as we regulate these tools and the fruits they provide.</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p><a href="https://x.com/Steve_Yegge/status/2094947586373505122">Steve Yegge:</a></p>

<blockquote>
  <p>All models, no matter how smart, will eventually build systems that they can no longer understand or maintain, if you let them. Fable 5 finally outbuilt itself, and flailed on me for a week. Fable 5.1 looks like it will fix it. For now. But you have to keep an iron grip on system size, or it’ll run away from you.</p>
</blockquote>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>I was going through some slightly-related work and discovered that the Creating Passionate Users blog had disappeared from the internet (and has been gone since maybe a year ago). For those who don’t know, <strong>Creating Passionate Users</strong> was one of the treasures of the Golden Age of internet blogging. It was the work of <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathy_Sierra">Kathy Sierra</a>, also known for co-creating the “Head First” series of computer books. It talked about user experience, and remains some of the best writing on the topic, full of sparkling insights that greatly influenced my thinking, as well as many folks more engaged on user-experience work.</p>

<p>Sadly not just was the blog ahead of time in its content, it was a harbinger of the darker side of the internet, as Kathy came under attack from a particularly virulent form of <a href="https://martinfowler.com/bliki/NetNastiness.html">Net Nastiness</a>. That led her to retreat from active participation on the web, and we’ve missed her ever since.</p>

<p>Fortunately the Wayback Machine did its great duty, and <a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20250909191913/https://headrush.typepad.com/creating_passionate_users/">we can still read its snapshot</a>. I’ve often thought that, if I had a clone to spare, I’d like to create a guided tour of Creating Passionate Users to help readers today read that excellent material. (And if you’re reading this Kathy, and want it still hosted on the web, I’d be delighted to.)</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p><a href="https://bsky.app/profile/simonwillison.net/post/3muwzopsoqs26">Simon Willison:</a></p>

<blockquote>
  <p>“I don’t know the answer myself, but I asked a blowhard I know and he took a wild guess, here’s what he said: “</p>

  <p>How I interpret pasted replies from an LLM in online conversations</p>
</blockquote>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>Jessica Kerr loves the feeling of being part of a team of people that learns from each other and from the codebase they are building as extensions of themselves - she incorporates the term <a href="https://jessitron.com/2018/04/15/the-origins-of-opera-and-the-future-of-programming/">symmathesy</a> for this: a learning system composed of learning parts (both the people and the code).</p>

<p><a href="https://jessitron.com/2026/08/30/who-are-we-now/">“But now agents!”</a></p>

<blockquote>
  <p>There was a turning point last year where I noticed that not only are they useful, it is irresponsible not to use them, at least in conjunction with my own code. They’re more thorough, as well as faster. How am I supposed to be responsible for this system, when I don’t understand each line of code?</p>
</blockquote>

<p>She has a habit of digging out old terms and ideas and applying them to our digital world. To frame what’s happening, she digs out two bits of latin</p>

<blockquote>
  <ul>
    <li>Verum Factum: I made it, so I get it</li>
    <li>Vexationes Artium: Put it to the test [i.e. experiments]</li>
  </ul>
</blockquote>

<p>Agents can’t have Verum Factum knowledge, since it’s gone once their context window clears. They can use Vexationes Artium, running tests to see if something is working.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>If we want agents to write working, reliable code for us, we have to double down, 10x down on our objective verification. We need to vexate that code in artful ways. And we have the agent help us with that, with its thoroughness.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>This is, of course, true of those building these AI models - they certainly don’t have a Verum Factum knowledge of how they work, all they can do is come up with artful vexations to figure out what might be going on in there.</p>

<p>What does that mean for us humans? Kerr says The Enlightenment elevated the idea that reason was the special quality of mankind. But now we’ve built machines that can reason. We need to focus instead on human qualities that the machines don’t have. Imagination is more important to us now than reason. And the essence of our humanity is in our relationships with other people.</p>

<p>This material was put together for a conference talk, it’s available in <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nvr0klVxw5c">video</a> and in <a href="https://jessitron.com/2026/08/30/who-are-we-now/">text</a> form. Generally I far prefer text, but there’s so many delights in her talk that I found it worth watching. (Such as her current definition of legacy software, as code that wasn’t built with agents in mind - and is thus lacking in testability and consistency.)</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>Jim Gumbly outlines the decision space for <a href="https://www.jimgumbley.com/blog/timing-tradeoff-skynet-timeline.html">three people who are making vital judgments</a> about controls and regulations for AI: Sam Altman, Dario Amodei, and David Sacks (for US government).</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Sacks worries that binding oversight could weaken American leadership and the financing that sustains it. Sam and Dario face commercial incentives to keep producing more capable models. The value of those models increasingly comes from persistence, cooperation and permission to act. Those same properties increase the demands on containment and oversight. Some advances also make the evidence harder to interpret.</p>

  <p>Each decision can be understandable on its own. Their combined effect can still move deployment ahead of our ability to control it.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>A complication for all this is the issue of observability of these models.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>OpenAI reports that Astra is better aligned on its evaluations while also being less monitorable. Its traces can be shorter and less informative. In adversarial tests, where it was instructed to evade oversight, it could conceal some behaviour from monitors.</p>

  <p>[…]</p>

  <p>That matters to the familiar software cycle of releasing something, observing what happens and improving it. The cycle depends on the quality of the observations. Fewer warning flags are reassuring only to the extent that the warning system remains capable of detecting the relevant failures.</p>
</blockquote>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>There’s an El Niño year coming up, and The Grauniad reports that climate scientists predict <a href="https://www.theguardian.com/environment/2026/sep/03/el-nino-supersizing-before-our-eyes-united-nations?CMP=share_btn_url">this El Niño is going to be a spectacularly hot one.</a></p>

<blockquote>
  <p>The most recent data, from Monday, shows the temperature of the ocean at the heart of El Niño at 2.6C above the 30-year average. That is already close to the highest anomaly ever recorded in the satellite data era, 3.1C in 2015, with months to go before the peak is expected.</p>

  <p>That peak is forecast to reach about 4C in November, according to the average of 14 different models. Data from analysis of corals, tree rings and historical documents suggest no El Niño has reached this level in the last millennium, said Zeke Hausfather, a climate analyst.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>If these forecasts end up being accurate, will this make a difference to how seriously people are taking the climate crisis?</p>

### [v0.30.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.30.0)

**来源**: vLLM GitHub Releases

**摘要**: <h1>v0.30.0</h1>
<h2>Highlights</h2>
<p>This release features 762 commits from 315 contributors (104 new)!</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56214">#56214</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56228">#56228</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56208">#56208</a>) with the whole KV stored in MXFP8 through the FlashMLA V4.1 record on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56893">#56893</a>), DeepGEMM Mega-mHC (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56962">#56962</a>), and async Engram prefetch with Engram DP sharding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56512">#56512</a>); DeepSeek-V4-Flash-Vision-Exp (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54566">#54566</a>), also on ROCm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55107">#55107</a>) and with LoRA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55897">#55897</a>); GLM-5.3-Flash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53906">#53906</a>) with EPLB (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55119">#55119</a>); K2-Horizon (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55063">#55063</a>); Cohere Compass (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54774">#54774</a>); Bailing V3 VL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55921">#55921</a>); Nanbeige4.2 via the Transformers backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56071">#56071</a>); and a DeepSeek-V4 CPU backend with AVX512/AMX sparse MLA, indexer, mHC and compressor kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55355">#55355</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Fast Start</strong>: a persistent per-GPU weight-cache daemon holds post-quantized, TP-sharded weights in GPU memory so restarting engines map them over CUDA IPC with <code>--load-format ipc_cache</code> instead of reloading from disk (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54921">#54921</a>), now covering FP4 checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55465">#55465</a>) and multi-node TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55468">#55468</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Watermarking</strong>: Gumbel-max watermarked generation and detection with a keyed PRF, per-request opt-out and an example detection endpoint (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54053">#54053</a>); dual-key Gumbel-max makes it compatible with speculative decoding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56122">#56122</a>); the Rust frontend forwards the per-request controls (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56338">#56338</a>).</li>
<li><strong>HiSparse</strong>: a host-resident tier for sparse-MLA decode that spills KV pages to pinned host memory under GPU pressure and serves top-k misses from a per-request GPU hot buffer, enabled through <code>HiSparseConnector</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53781">#53781</a>), with Prometheus counters (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56061">#56061</a>), a host cache shared across TP ranks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56629">#56629</a>), and the attention config inferred from the connector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57041">#57041</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2</strong>: dual-batch overlap in eager mode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50945">#50945</a>) and with FULL CUDA graphs for microbatched steps (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51700">#51700</a>); MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46994">#46994</a>) and EAGLE3/DFlash/DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50514">#50514</a>) speculative decoding under pipeline parallelism; adaptive verification for every draft-model speculator through an online acceptance estimator (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52228">#52228</a>); gc frozen during graph capture, cutting capture from 12s to 2s and engine init from 28.9s to 8.2s on H200 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54646">#54646</a>); <code>--return-sampling-mask</code> compacted on GPU, fixing an about 2x RL step-time regression (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54901">#54901</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Qwen3.8-Flash-Next performance</strong>: separate prefill and decode QSA indexer kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54513">#54513</a>), fused PLE kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54517">#54517</a>), FP8 indexer cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54890">#54890</a>), padded-index skipping in sparse GQA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54873">#54873</a>), fused PLE residual and QSA output gate (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55309">#55309</a>), UVA PLE offload and Engram tensor parallelism via <code>--engram-config</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54371">#54371</a>), and torch.compile removed from the NVIDIA implementation so FP8 fits on a single GB300 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55272">#55272</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kimi K3 performance</strong>: native CUDA AttnRes default on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54261">#54261</a>), KDA mixed-batch gather/scatter removed (5.2-7.7% E2E throughput, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56159">#56159</a>), grouped FP8 MLA cache insertion (4-6x kernel speedup at small batch, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55356">#55356</a>), DSV3 low-latency GEMM on strided tensors (12-81% kernel speedup, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54565">#54565</a>), overlapped TP8 KDA projections (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54697">#54697</a>), FlashInfer KDA kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55364">#55364</a>), internal prefix checkpoints with partial prefix caching and speculative decoding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53614">#53614</a>), and symmetric DCP disaggregation for hybrid Mamba models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55531">#55531</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Large scale serving</strong>: PCP+DCP on sparse-MLA models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56157">#56157</a>), PCP with single-module MTP and replicated DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56107">#56107</a>) and decode-only FULL CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53867">#53867</a>), Elastic EP reusing CUDA graphs across reconfiguration (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54985">#54985</a>), an opt-in FlashInfer PCIe IPC all-reduce for NVLink-less boxes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53576">#53576</a>), DeepEP v2 async finalize overlapping shared experts with combine (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52781">#52781</a>), Mooncake Store heterogeneous TP sharing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53129">#53129</a>), a KVCR secondary-tier adapter (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53624">#53624</a>), and encoder-cache sharing over NIXL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47941">#47941</a>) and Mooncake (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/41567">#41567</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Quantization</strong>: targeted online quantization through <code>quantization_config.targets</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51285">#51285</a>) and on partially pre-quantized checkpoints from any quant method (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51392">#51392</a>), W4A16 DSA with the <code>nvfp4_fp8_ds_mla</code> KV cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51724">#51724</a>), FlashInfer CuTeDSL NVFP4 W4A16 default over Marlin on SM100/103 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53014">#53014</a>), NVFP4 in the torch linear backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53319">#53319</a>), per-quantization linear backend overrides (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51204">#51204</a>), AutoRound 2/3/5/6/7-bit on CUDA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52890">#52890</a>), and DeepSelect top-k for the DSA sparse indexer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56464">#56464</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Breaking changes</strong>: scale-out endpoints are opt-in on plain <code>vllm serve</code> via <code>--enable-scale-out</code>, replacing <code>VLLM_ENABLE_SCALE_OUT_ENDPOINTS</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54579">#54579</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55176">#55176</a>); GPTQ activation ordering (<code>g_idx</code>) removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54809">#54809</a>); items deprecated for 0.29 removed, including the <code>VLLM_PREFIX_CACHE_RETENTION_INTERVAL</code> and <code>VLLM_MM_HASHER_ALGORITHM</code> env vars (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55353">#55353</a>); the <code>all</code> Mamba cache mode deprecated (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55041">#55041</a>); <code>python -m vllm.entrypoints.grpc_server</code> deprecated in favor of <code>vllm serve --grpc</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56746">#56746</a>); YaRN aligned with Transformers so vendor YaRN aliases no longer re-scale <code>max_model_len</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56446">#56446</a>).</li>
</ul>

<li>Source distribution tarball</li>

<h2>Model Support</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56214">#56214</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56228">#56228</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56208">#56208</a>), DeepSeek-V4-Flash-Vision-Exp (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54566">#54566</a>) with ROCm support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55107">#55107</a>) and LoRA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55897">#55897</a>), GLM-5.3-Flash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53906">#53906</a>), K2-Horizon with its reasoning and tool parsers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55063">#55063</a>), Cohere Compass (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54774">#54774</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55949">#55949</a>), Bailing V3 VL with MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55921">#55921</a>), Nanbeige4.2 via the Transformers backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56071">#56071</a>), and a DeepSeek-V4 CPU backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55355">#55355</a>).</li>
<li><strong>DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash</strong>: whole KV in MXFP8 through the FlashMLA V4.1 record on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56893">#56893</a>), Mega-mHC from the DeepGEMM fork (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56962">#56962</a>), mHC post block folded into the delayed pre projection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56633">#56633</a>), Triton-fused input metadata preparation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56562">#56562</a>), async prefetch for CPU-offloaded Engram lookups and Engram DP sharding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56512">#56512</a>), DSpark draft states collapsed before the SP all-gather (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56903">#56903</a>), DSpark drafter no longer inheriting uninitialized EPLB state (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56387">#56387</a>), XGrammar V4.1 schema constraints for strict tool parameters (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56408">#56408</a>), Responses text parts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56299">#56299</a>), and image sentinel padding scoped to the Vision-Exp checkpoint (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56554">#56554</a>).</li>
<li><strong>DeepSeek V4</strong>: plain RoPE without YaRN on sparse SWA layers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54815">#54815</a>), <code>--kv-cache-dtype auto</code> resolving to <code>fp8_ds_mla</code> with FlashMLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45091">#45091</a>), prefill sparse index workspace sentinel seeded (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55299">#55299</a>), dequant gather grid sized by rows (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55061">#55061</a>), optional Q-norm in the fused MLA epilogue and group_size=32 packed FP8 quant (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56215">#56215</a>), sparse settings read from the text config for composite models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56160">#56160</a>), MegaMoE startup without EP fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55914">#55914</a>), and a Triton iHC pre/post fallback for HY V4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55059">#55059</a>).</li>
<li><strong>GLM-5.3-Flash</strong>: EPLB support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55119">#55119</a>), FlashKDA for KDA chunked prefill (1.7-3.8x faster than the Triton chunk path, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55737">#55737</a>), dense and masked-MHA sparse prefill for the NoPE (256, 0, 256) layout (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55738">#55738</a>), decode hot-path cleanups removing per-layer copies and a duplicate router GEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55736">#55736</a>), and the <code>glm5next</code> NVIDIA subtree now packaged in wheels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55214">#55214</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Qwen3.8-Flash-Next</strong>: separate prefill and decode QSA indexer kernels with <code>VLLM_SPARSE_INDEXER_MAX_LOGITS_MB</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54513">#54513</a>), fused PLE kernels and merged K/V projections (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54517">#54517</a>), sparse GQA skipping padded indices (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54873">#54873</a>), FP8 indexer cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54890">#54890</a>), fused PLE residual and QSA output gate (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55309">#55309</a>), UVA PLE offload and Engram tensor parallelism via <code>--engram-config</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54371">#54371</a>), torch.compile removed from the NVIDIA path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55272">#55272</a>), compact indexer logits workspace (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54687">#54687</a>), PLE MTP metadata transfers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55054">#55054</a>), Hopper LL-GEMM tuning table (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54560">#54560</a>), B200 FP8 MoE tuning (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55890">#55890</a>), and fixes for FP8 PLE weight scales (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54722">#54722</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54882">#54882</a>) and fused PLE conv strides (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55375">#55375</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kimi K3</strong>: native CUDA AttnRes default on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54261">#54261</a>), ll_bf16 router weight prefetch (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53524">#53524</a>), overlapped TP8 KDA projections (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54697">#54697</a>), strided DSV3 GEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54565">#54565</a>), MLA decode epilogue latency (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54896">#54896</a>), NVFP4 in_proj alignment routing to DeepGEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55242">#55242</a>), mixed-batch KDA without gather/scatter (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56159">#56159</a>), grouped FP8 MLA cache insertion (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55356">#55356</a>), FlashInfer KDA kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55364">#55364</a>), FlashKDA bf16 checkpoint state (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56485">#56485</a>) and a stability bump (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54859">#54859</a>), SiTU on the FlashInfer CuTeDSL MoE backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54606">#54606</a>), internal prefix checkpoints with partial prefix caching and spec decoding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53614">#53614</a>), and fixes for RecoverSSM startup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54634">#54634</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55774">#55774</a>), GDN metadata (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54636">#54636</a>), mixed-batch masks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54781">#54781</a>), checkpoint indexing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55747">#55747</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56794">#56794</a>), KDA illegal memory access (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55924">#55924</a>), interleaved weight streams (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53379">#53379</a>), NVFP4 MoE conversion OOM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55407">#55407</a>), and KDA projection overlap on Hopper (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55426">#55426</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Transformers backend</strong>: attention found with a fuser and vLLM's layer attached to it (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54941">#54941</a>), generic merged-column linear fusion (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55301">#55301</a>), QKV fuser for Gemma4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55690">#55690</a>), all vocab embeddings replaced including Gemma 3n/4 per-layer tables (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54760">#54760</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55083">#55083</a>), and attention sinks applied (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52156">#52156</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding for models</strong>: EAGLE3 for Sarvam MLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53052">#53052</a>), Nemotron-H MTP with a separate, possibly quantized lm_head (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54574">#54574</a>), NVFP4 DSpark gathered top-k projection for quantized Markov heads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55713">#55713</a>), Qwen3.5/3.6 multimodal MTP resolving <code>n_predict</code> from the text config (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55369">#55369</a>), Qwen3 DSpark padded-vocab drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55133">#55133</a>), and GLM-OCR MTP weight loading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49869">#49869</a>) and position masking under CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56447">#56447</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal</strong>: torchcodec audio decoding and a selectable <code>audio_backend</code> in <code>--media-io-kwargs</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51826">#51826</a>) with automatic decoding kept soundfile-first (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55642">#55642</a>), torchaudio as the default resampler (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52598">#52598</a>), <code>media_io_kwargs</code> in multimodal hashes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54241">#54241</a>), cache hash kwargs scoped by modality (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54918">#54918</a>), empty video URLs with multimodal UUIDs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54220">#54220</a>), cached audio inputs with UUIDs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56310">#56310</a>), encoder CUDA graphs for MiniCPM-V 2.5/2.6/4.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42785">#42785</a>), Voxtral Realtime with <code>FULL_DECODE_ONLY</code> graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51167">#51167</a>), Triton/FlashInfer composite attention for multimodal prefixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56305">#56305</a>), pruned sliding-window tiles for Gemma 4 multimodal prompts (up to 3-4x E2E, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53147">#53147</a>), SDPA for BLIP-2 Q-Former (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55285">#55285</a>), baddbmm Conformer scores (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55062">#55062</a>), fused DeepEncoder relative bias (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55629">#55629</a>), Gemma3n sparse GELU Triton kernel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48498">#48498</a>), and no duplicate text embedding in Qwen2.5-Omni (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55415">#55415</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Correctness</strong>: Gemma 4 / Gemma 3n fine-tuned checkpoints without KV projections on shared layers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54917">#54917</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55864">#55864</a>), Gemma4 video preprocessing without image kwargs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56652">#56652</a>) and unified audio profiling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56721">#56721</a>), MiniCPM-V 4.6 loading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49417">#49417</a>) and video embeds (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54633">#54633</a>), InternVL video parser (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55779">#55779</a>), Nano-Nemotron precomputed embeddings (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56398">#56398</a>), OpenPangu multimodal merge (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55941">#55941</a>) and sleep mode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53696">#53696</a>), Qwen3-VL and Cosmos3-Edge under CPU and PP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53699">#53699</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43272">#43272</a>), Qwen VL processors without a tokenizer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54886">#54886</a>), Molmo2 fps guard (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55893">#55893</a>), CohereASR audio-token estimate (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53829">#53829</a>), double BOS in <code>LLM.chat()</code> for multimodal models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55288">#55288</a>), decoded frame lists parsed as one video (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55326">#55326</a>), swapped H/W in dummy video profiling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56385">#56385</a>), base64 video validation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54323">#54323</a>), Sentence Transformers 5.4+ configs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54533">#54533</a>), ColQwen3.5 pooler projector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54847">#54847</a>), torch.compile enabled for StableLM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54969">#54969</a>) and Sarvam MLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55817">#55817</a>), and Nemotron latent-MoE skipping a redundant all-reduce at TP&gt;1 (about 13% decode win, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52301">#52301</a>).</li>
<li><strong>LoRA</strong>: rsLoRA scaling in MoE expert packing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55548">#55548</a>), a log line when an adapter applies no weights (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55310">#55310</a>), and <code>--lora-modules name=path</code> with <code>=</code> in the path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53353">#53353</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Engine Core</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2</strong>: DBO in eager mode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50945">#50945</a>) and with FULL CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51700">#51700</a>), MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46994">#46994</a>) and EAGLE3-style drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50514">#50514</a>) under pipeline parallelism, adaptive verification for all draft-model speculators (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52228">#52228</a>), gc frozen during capture (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54646">#54646</a>), GPU-compacted sampling masks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54901">#54901</a>), UVA-backed <code>apply_write</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55819">#55819</a>), fast-prefill support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56145">#56145</a>), DP sync skipped for all speculator uniform decodes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54856">#54856</a>), prompt embeddings in decoder pooling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56639">#56639</a>), pooling post-processing on non-final PP ranks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56666">#56666</a>), kernels warmed before graph capture (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55341">#55341</a>), <code>CUDAGraphStat</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52358">#52358</a>), Proton CUDA graph attribution (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51084">#51084</a>), platforms without pinned memory such as WSL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56908">#56908</a>), draft <code>moe_backend</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54788">#54788</a>) and <code>attention_backend</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54826">#54826</a>) overrides honored, DCP metadata initialized after batch partitioning (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55212">#55212</a>), and a <code>record_stream</code> fix in the PP draft broadcast (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55745">#55745</a>). The MRV2-specific Triton JIT warmup migration was reverted (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56654">#56654</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: opt-in <code>disable_eagle_block_drop</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53388">#53388</a>), adaptive verification on <code>FLASHINFER_MLA_SPARSE_DSV4</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52724">#52724</a>) and deferred until after kernel warmup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55455">#55455</a>), full-vocab EAGLE3 drafters skipping vocab remapping (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53458">#53458</a>), DSpark preserving the target DCP config (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55472">#55472</a>), DFlash drafters dropping FlashAttention's AOT schedule (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54374">#54374</a>), DP token padding excluded from draft metadata (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55458">#55458</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56181">#56181</a>), fastsafetensors deadlock avoided for PP drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54416">#54416</a>), Mamba state cached at the EAGLE resume position (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53945">#53945</a>) and preserved for padded prompt tails (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55178">#55178</a>), resumed requests padded in P/D (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55126">#55126</a>), and warmup device selection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55245">#55245</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Prefix caching and scheduling</strong>: both replay boundaries retained so an EAGLE resend of a block-aligned prompt still hits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54713">#54713</a>), zero-progress preemption cascades stopped for deferred KV frees (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49675">#49675</a>), SWA layers taking the primary block size to avoid inflating the KV block LCM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53007">#53007</a>), align-mode Mamba states retired across null gaps (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55450">#55450</a>), the <code>all</code> Mamba cache mode deprecated with MRV1 fallback (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55041">#55041</a>), and <code>vllm:request_num_preemptions</code> histogram (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49984">#49984</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kernels</strong>: FlashInfer ReplaySSM backend for Mamba2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52506">#52506</a>), B12X causal paged attention for SM120/SM121 via <code>--attention-backend B12X_ATTN</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52017">#52017</a>), FlashInfer XQA decode on SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50439">#50439</a>), DeepSelect top-k with <code>--sparse-indexer-topk-backend</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56464">#56464</a>), BF16x3 router GEMM default on SM100 for FP32-router models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55899">#55899</a>), split-row Triton top-p for small batches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54651">#54651</a>), manual <code>ActivationQuantFusionPass</code> producer for Llama (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51415">#51415</a>), fused add-RMSNorm + NVFP4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51925">#51925</a>), prefix-prefill tiling independent of KV page size (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54194">#54194</a>), FlashInfer GDN prefill on SM12x (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55715">#55715</a>), fused GDN MTP decode for SM110 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53835">#53835</a>), masked MoE activation for padded layouts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51217">#51217</a>), cooperative <code>batched_moe_align_block_size</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53280">#53280</a>), PDL in <code>fusedQKNormRopeKernel</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55755">#55755</a>) and weight prefetch in fused q/kv RMSNorm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55020">#55020</a>), XD-RoPE folded into M-RoPE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56078">#56078</a>), and the FlashAttention pin synced with upstream (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54819">#54819</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Startup and warmup</strong>: renderer warmup overlapped with engine core init (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54557">#54557</a>), FlashInfer autotune no longer rerun on each source change (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54794">#54794</a>), Qwen3.5/Qwen3-Next Triton kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54797">#54797</a>) and GDN gated RMSNorm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54251">#54251</a>) warmed at startup, Gemma 4 de-JITified (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55768">#55768</a>), DeepSeek V4 and shared attention, sampling, DFlash and mHC kernels migrated to the JIT warmup registry (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50175">#50175</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53564">#53564</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53565">#53565</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50176">#50176</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53566">#53566</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56323">#56323</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50178">#50178</a>) with parallel mHC pre-norm compilation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56683">#56683</a>), FlashInfer deferred MoE decode kernels autotuned before capture (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55377">#55377</a>), and supplemental BF16 autotuning isolated (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57285">#57285</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Fast Start</strong>: weight-cache daemon with <code>--load-format ipc_cache</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54921">#54921</a>), FP4 checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55465">#55465</a>), multi-node TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55468">#55468</a>), GPU UUID socket folders (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56669">#56669</a>), per-client IPC tensor export so a copy-mode client cannot release zero-copy weights (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56472">#56472</a>), and a clear error on non-CUDA/ROCm platforms (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56010">#56010</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Watermarking</strong>: Gumbel-max watermarked generation and detection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54053">#54053</a>) and dual-key Gumbel-max for speculative decoding with <code>allow_target_only_watermarking</code> and context deduplication (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56122">#56122</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Sleep and RL</strong>: NCCL communicator memory released in sleep mode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51485">#51485</a>), DP state synced on the first step of a wave so pause and sleep stop burning 32 dummy forwards per rank (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52957">#52957</a>), pre-shared <code>ncclUniqueId</code> rendezvous for torch-free trainers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53784">#53784</a>), stale HPC QK-norm weights after refit fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56706">#56706</a>), and <code>session_id</code> on GPU <code>BlockStored</code> events (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51381">#51381</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Robustness</strong>: attention implementations must explicitly declare DCP support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55780">#55780</a>), unavailable piecewise CUDA graphs raise instead of producing garbled output (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54782">#54782</a>), non-compiled models fall back to <code>FULL_DECODE_ONLY</code> graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55095">#55095</a>), MRV1 breakable graphs scoped to the piecewise path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56312">#56312</a>), sync checks cover paged async copies (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53491">#53491</a>) and torch.compile (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56904">#56904</a>), more H2D copies pinned (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54660">#54660</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55202">#55202</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56138">#56138</a>), GDN capture metadata built without a device sync (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55404">#55404</a>), queued work carried over when materializing the dedicated stream (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56382">#56382</a>), FlexAttention recompiles avoided (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55888">#55888</a>), persistent top-k falling back on low-shared-memory GPUs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54110">#54110</a>), KV page size scaled for hidden-state extraction with TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50894">#50894</a>), out-of-bounds access in SM90 sparse MLA mixed batches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56969">#56969</a>), sparse-MLA piecewise capture crash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56825">#56825</a>), TRT-LLM ragged prefill regression (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55499">#55499</a>), MoE fused sum int32 overflow (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50220">#50220</a>), odd-row per-token-group quant cliff (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56478">#56478</a>), dummy-load re-initialization (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56682">#56682</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56688">#56688</a>), <code>warning_once</code> retaining the LLM instance (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54098">#54098</a>), DSpark cache-group capability under <code>python -O</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55234">#55234</a>), sparse-MLA DCP stabilized for GLM PCP configurations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55879">#55879</a>), DeepGEMM FP8 warmup coverage (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56452">#56452</a>), Triton autotune inspection skipped without Triton (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56676">#56676</a>), <code>mm_device_do_normalize</code> under encoder CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55370">#55370</a>), VidCom2 frame budgets read once (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55331">#55331</a>), the CUDA profiler start fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55237">#55237</a>), FlashInfer all-reduce workspace failures logged as errors (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55127">#55127</a>), and backend-neutral KV cache and MFU log lines (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55650">#55650</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Hardware &amp; Performance</h2>
